TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD)\n   159→    mean_y = float(np.nanmean(y[visible_mask])) if np.any(visible_mask) else float(np.nanmean(y))\n   160→    occluded_mask = ~visible_mask\n   161→    y[occluded_mask] = mean_y\n   162→\n   163→    diagnostics = {\n   164→        \"speed_threshold\": speed_threshold,\n   165→        \"n_velocity_outliers\": int(np.sum(outlier_mask)),\n   166→        \"n_low_likelihood_frames\": int(np.sum(occluded_mask)),\n   167→        \"mean_visible_y\": mean_y,\n   168→    }\n   169→    return y, diagnostics\n   170→\n   171→\n   172→def align_tongue_y(\n   173→    timestamps: np.ndarray,\n   174→    cleaned_y: np.ndarray,\n   175→    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   176→) -> np.ndarray:\n   177→    abs_centers = go_times[:, None] + REL_CENTERS[None, :]\n   178→    idx = np.searchsorted(timestamps, abs_centers, side=\"right\") - 1\n   179→    idx = np.clip(idx, 0, len(timestamps) - 1)\n   180→    return cleaned_y[idx]\n   181→\n   182→\n   183→def build_choice_array(\n   184→    trial_instruction: np.ndarray,\n   185→    outcome_code: np.ndarray,\n   186→    start_times: np.ndarray,\n   187→    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   188→    stop_times: np.ndarray,\n   189→    left_lick_times: np.ndarray,\n   190→    right_lick_times: np.ndarray,\n   191→) -> np.ndarray:\n   192→    choice = np.zeros(len(trial_instruction), dtype=np.int16)\n   193→    instructed = np.where(trial_instruction == \"left\", 0, 1).astype(np.int16)\n   194→\n   195→    hit_mask = outcome_code == 2\n   196→    miss_mask = outcome_code == 1\n   197→    ignore_mask = outcome_code == 0\n   198→\n   199→    choice[hit_mask] = instructed[hit_mask]\n   200→    choice[miss_mask] = 1 - instructed[miss_mask]\n   201→\n   202→    ignore_trials = np.where(ignore_mask)[0]\n   203→    for trial_idx in ignore_trials:\n   204→        choice[trial_idx] = lick_choice_with_fallback(\n   205→            left_times=left_lick_times,\n   206→            right_times=right_lick_times,\n   207→            start_time=float(start_times[trial_idx]),\n   208→            go_time=float(go_times[trial_idx]),\n   209→            stop_time=float(stop_times[trial_idx]),\n   210→            instructed_choice=int(instructed[trial_idx]),\n   211→        )\n   212→    return choice\n   213→\n   214→\n   215→def build_photostim_matrix(\n   216→    photostim_onset_str: np.ndarray,\n   217→    photostim_duration_str: np.ndarray,\n   218→    start_times: np.ndarray,\n   219→    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   220→) -> np.ndarray:\n   221→    onset_trial = parse_optional_float_array(photostim_onset_str)\n   222→    duration = parse_optional_float_array(photostim_duration_str)\n   223→    go_minus_start = go_times - start_times\n   224→    onset_rel_go = onset_trial - go_minus_start\n   225→    offset_rel_go = onset_rel_go + duration\n   226→\n   227→    stim = np.zeros((len(go_times), N_BINS), dtype=np.float16)\n   228→    valid = np.isfinite(onset_rel_go) & np.isfinite(offset_rel_go)\n   229→    for trial_idx in np.where(valid)[0]:\n   230→        mask = (REL_CENTERS >= onset_rel_go[trial_idx]) & (REL_CENTERS < offset_rel_go[trial_idx])\n   231→        stim[trial_idx, mask] = 1.0\n   232→    return stim\n   233→\n   234→\n   235→def bin_spikes_to_firing_rates(\n   236→    spike_times_flat: np.ndarray,\n   237→    spike_times_index: np.ndarray,\n   238→    good_unit_indices: np.ndarray,\n   239→    trial_edges_abs: np.ndarray,\n   240→) -> np.ndarray:\n   241→    n_trials = trial_edges_abs.shape[0]\n   242→    n_edges = trial_edges_abs.shape[1]\n   243→    flat_edges = trial_edges_abs.reshape(-1)\n   244→    start_index = np.concatenate(([0], spike_times_index[:-1]))\n   245→    firing_rates = np.empty((len(good_unit_indices), n_trials, n_edges - 1), dtype=np.float16)\n   246→\n   247→    for i, unit_idx in enumerate(good_unit_indices):\n   248→        spikes = spike_times_flat[start_index[unit_idx]: spike_times_index[unit_idx]]\n   249→        edge_idx = np.searchsorted(spikes, flat_edges, side=\"left\").reshape(n_trials, n_edges)\n   250→        counts = np.diff(edge_idx, axis=1)\n   251→        firing_rates[i] = (counts / BIN_SIZE_S).astype(np.float16)\n   252→    return firing_rates\n   253→\n   254→\n   255→def compute_valid_trial_mask(\n   256→    obs_intervals: np.ndarray,\n   257→    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   258→) -> np.ndarray:\n   259→    trial_start = go_times + REL_START_S\n   260→    trial_end = go_times + REL_END_S\n   261→    starts = obs_intervals[:, 0][None, :]\n   262→    ends = obs_intervals[:, 1][None, :]\n   263→    return np.any((trial_start[:, None] >= starts) & (trial_end[:, None] <= ends), axis=1)\n   264→\n   265→\n   266→def make_session_plot(\n   267→    result: SessionResult,\n   268→    out_path: Path,\n   269→) -> None:\n   270→    diag = result.diagnostics\n   271→ plt.close(fig)\n   315→\n   316→\n   317→def process_session(path: Path) -> SessionResult:\n   318→    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n   319→    session_id = path.stem\n   320→    subject_id = 499→\n   500→\n   501→def build_dataset(results: list[SessionResult]) -> dict:\n   502→    subjects = []\n   503→    subject_to_idx = {}\n   504→    brain_regions = []\n   505→    region_to_idx = {}\n \"metadata\": {\n   524→            \"task_description\": (\n   525→                \"Auditory delayed-response task aligned to go cue.   \"excluded_sessions\": [ZERO_GOOD_SESSION],\n   539→            \"unit_filter\": \"units/classification == 'good'\",\n   540→            \"tone_onset_definition\": \"Canonical sample onset at -1.85 data[\"brain_region_idx\"].append(session_region_idx)\n   574→\n   575→    return data\n   576→\n   577→\n   578→def main() -> None:\n   579→    args = parse_args()\n   580→    sample_only = response | 0.99 ± 0.01 s.d., n=106 sessions | “Soon after the go cue, prediction saturated … 0.99 ± 0.01” |\n   130→\n   131→\n   132→### Processing Details\n   133→- Behavioral task structure from `methods.txt` / datapaper:\n   134→  - Sample epoch: three 150 ms tones with 100 ms inter-tone intervals.\n   135→  - Delay epoch: `1.2 s`.\n   136→  - Go cue: `0.1 s` auditory cue marking end of delay.\n   137→  - Response epoch / answer period: `1.5 s`.\n   138→  - Consumption period: `1.5 s`.\n   139→  - Trial ends after `1.5 s` without licking, followed by `250 ms` ITI.\n   140→- Temporal alignment convention in the reference preprocessing is go-cue-centered. The code subtracts go cue time from lick times and stimulation times, and the papers discuss peri-go-cue timing throughout (`Time to go (s)`).\n   141→- Photoinhibition timing: late-delay ALM photoinhibition lasts the final `0.5 s` of delay including `100 ms` ramp-down and ends before the go cue.\n   142→- Method paper preprocessing for the video/neural analysis:\n   143→  - Spikes converted to firing rates using `40 ms` bins and `3.4 ms` stride.\n   144→  - Only side-view video frames were used.\n   145→  - DeepLabCut markers included jaw, paws, and tongue.\n   146→  - Marker outliers were identified using a five-sigma frame-to-frame velocity rule and imputed from nearby frames.\n   147→  - Tongue position was imputed to its mean when occluded before the response epoch.\n   148→- For PSTHs in the datapaper, spike counts were binned at `1 ms` then boxcar-averaged over `200 ms`; that is for figure generation, not the main preprocessed dataset used by the method paper.\n   149→\n   150→### Curation Steps\n   151→\n   152→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   153→- Spike sorting performed with Kilosort2; each cluster assigned 15 quality metrics.\n   154→- Region-specific logistic-regression classifiers were trained from manually curated labels in five major area groups (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla).\n   155→- The classifier outputs defined the “good” units used in the papers.\n   156→- Region mapping from `methods.txt`:\n   157→  - Cortex classifier: ALM, other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory, cortical subplate.\n   158→  - Striatum classifier: striatum, pallidum.\n   159→  - Thalamus classifier: thalamus, hypothalamus.\n   160→  - Midbrain classifier: midbrain, pons.\n   161→  - Medulla classifier: medulla, cerebellum.\n   162→- False alarm rates on held-out manually labeled units: cortex `7.8%`, striatum `6.4%`, thalamus `7.3%`, midbrain `5.5%`, medulla `4.3%`.\n   163→\n   164→**Trial curation rules**:\n   165→- Early lick trials and no-response trials were excluded for analysis in the datapaper.\n   166→- Overall behavioral performance for session inclusion was computed on control trials only, excluding early licks.\n   167→- Sessions were selected for analysis with:\n   168→  - performance `> 65%`\n   169→  - at least `50` correct lick-left and `50` correct lick-right trials.\n   170→- Many downstream analyses in the method paper further use only correct trials, or only non-stimulation “regular” trials, depending on the analysis.\n   171→\n   172→### Decoders Trained\n   173→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   174→| Choice from behavioral videos, pre-sample | ROC AUC `0.51 ± 0.06` (n=106 sessions) |\n   175→| Choice from behavioral videos, sample+delay | ROC AUC `0.66 ± 0.12` (n=106 sessions) |\n   176→| Choice from behavioral videos, response | ROC AUC `0.99 ± 0.01` (n=106 sessions) |\n   177→| Single-neuron choice / movement prediction during delay | ROC AUC used as metric in method paper; neurons considered modulated at AUC `> 0.6` in some analyses |\n   178→\n   179→---\n   180→\n   181→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   182→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   183→\n   184→### Discrepancies Found\n   185→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   186→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   187→| Session count | Reference preprocessing / papers operate on analyzed sessions; code paths and text consistently refer to `173` behavioral sessions | Local NWB release contains `174` raw sessions | Datapaper / methods: `173 behavioral sessions` | Exactly one raw session (`sub-440958_ses-20190216T162508_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb`) has zero units with `classification == good`. Excluding that session yields `173` sessions, matching the papers. |\n   188→| Probe insertion count | Reference text methods: `655 probe insertions`; datapaper figure caption: `660 penetrations` | Raw NWB HDF5 electrode-group count is `659` insertions across 174 sessions; excluding the zero-good-unit session yields `655` | Figure caption says `660 penetrations`, STAR Methods says `655 probe insertions` | Use `655` as the analyzed-set count because it matches the code/paper analyzed session set after excluding the zero-good-unit session. Treat `660` as a figure-caption inconsistency or broader raw-count convention. |\n   189→| Good-unit total | Reference code uses external classifier-produced `goodunits` files; whitepaper/papers report `69,943` good units | NWB `classification == good` gives `69,453` units | Papers: `69,943 good units` | Small residual mismatch of `490` units remains. Most likely causes are release/version differences or a difference between the external `goodunits` files used by the reference code and the NWB `classification` field. For NWB-native conversion, `classification == good` is the closest directly available analogue and matches the session / insertion counts after excluding the zero-good session. I will keep checking this later against decoder behavior and dataset statistics. |\n   190→| Trial filtering | `get_regular_trial_mask` excludes early lick, auto water, free water, no-response, and stimulation trials | Raw trials include all of these categories | Papers describe exclusion of early lick and no-response trials; some downstream analyses also restrict to correct or control-only trials | Distinguish three levels: raw trial table, paper session-selection criteria, and stricter downstream “regular trial” filters. The conversion should preserve requested outputs and only apply exclusions required by the decoder task or by clear reference consistency. |\n   191→| Trial/event alignment | Reference code assumes go-cue-aligned exported spike/behavior variables and stores one trial row per trial | In NWB, `go_start_times` matches trial count exactly, but `sample_start_times` differs in `173/174` sessions and `delay_start_times` differs in `174/174` sessions because replayed epochs are stored explicitly | Papers state that early licks triggered replay of sample/delay epochs | Use `go_start_times` as the unique per-trial alignment anchor. When sample/delay timing is needed, use the final sample/delay event within each trial window rather than assuming one event per trial. |\n   192→| Photostim timing representation | Reference code stores stimulation times relative to go cue after subtracting go cue time | NWB trial table stores `photostim_onset` relative to trial start; in example trials, subtracting `(go_time - trial_start)` gives about `-1.2 s`, matching late-delay stimulation | Papers: photoinhibition in last `0.5 s` of delay, ending before go cue | Convert photostimulation onset/offset from trial-start coordinates into go-aligned coordinates during conversion. |\n   193→| Trial instruction coding | Reference preprocessing stores `trial_type = 1*(trial_type == 'l')` | NWB trial table stores strings `left` / `right` | Papers discuss lick-left / lick-right trial types | Map NWB `trial_instruction` to left/right categorical outputs directly; this is consistent with the reference code once recoded. |\n   194→\n   195→Final consistency understanding:\n   196→- The NWB files are the raw session-level source.\n   197→- The paper/code analyzed dataset is best approximated in NWB as: sessions with at least one good classified unit, using go-cue-centered alignment, and QC-filtered units corresponding to `classification == good`.\n   198→- `go_start_times` is the reliable one-per-trial event; sample/delay event streams need within-trial disambiguation because of replayed epochs.\n   199→- The method paper’s `40 ms` / `3.4 ms` neural preprocessing describes the reference analysis pipeline, but the requested decoder conversion will intentionally deviate to `50 ms` bins while preserving the same alignment and curation principles where possible.\n   200→\n   201→---\n   202→\n   203→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   204→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   205→\n   206→### Variable Mapping\n   207→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   208→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   209→| `units/spike_times`, `units/classification`, `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` | `neural` | Keep only units with `classification == \"good\"`; exclude the single zero-good-unit session; bin absolute spike times into go-aligned `[-2.5, 1.5)` windows using `50 ms` bins; convert counts to firing rates in Hz | `process_one_sess`, `process_one_area`, `sliding_histogram` | One trial array per trial, shape `(n_good_units, 80)` |\n   210→| Task structure from papers (`sample = 0.65 s`, `delay = 1.2 s`) + go cue | `input[0]` | Compute `time_from_tone_onset_s = bin_center - (-1.85 s)` for every bin | `process_one_sess` stores sample/delay periods; go-centered alignment throughout reference code | Raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events and are not reliable one-to-one trial markers; using the canonical `-1.85 s` tone onset is more consistent with the task definition |\n   211→| `intervals/trials/photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration`, `start_time`, `go_start_times` | `input[1]` | Convert photostim onset/duration from trial-start coordinates to go-centered coordinates; emit binary `0/1` per bin center for photostim on/off | `process_one_sess` aligns stimulation by subtracting go cue | Keep stimulation trials because photostim is an explicit decoder input |\n   212→| `intervals/trials/trial_instruction`, `intervals/trials/outcome`, lick events | `output[0]` (`choice`) | Encode left=`0`, right=`1`; for `hit`, use instructed side; for `miss`, use opposite side; for `ignore`, use first post-go lick side if present, else first lick side anywhere in trial if present, else fall back to instructed side | `process_one_sess` stores `trial_type`; lick-direction handling is implicit in exported behavior variables | Output is constant across time bins for a trial |\n   213→| `intervals/trials/outcome` | `output[1]` (`outcome`) | Map `ignore -> 0`, `miss -> 1`, `hit -> 2` | Paper/task variable; used in trial filtering logic throughout code | Constant across time bins |\n   214→| `intervals/trials/early_lick` | `output[2]` (`early_lick`) | Map `no early -> 0`, `early -> 1` | `get_regular_trial_mask` and datapaper methods | Constant across time bins |\n   215→| `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking` `(x, y, likelihood)` + timestamps | `output[3]` (`tongue_y_position`) | Clean tracking using method-paper-inspired velocity-outlier interpolation; align to go-centered bin centers using last-frame-carried-forward; compute per-session 40th/60th percentiles over all included aligned bins; discretize y into `0/1/2` | Method paper preprocessing + `align_markers_between_lims` | Time-varying output over 80 bins |\n   216→| `subject` folder / NWB subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Use exact subject IDs (`sub-xxxxx`) and map each session to its subject index | N/A | Session order will follow sorted NWB file paths |\n   217→| `units/anno_name` for good units | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Use unique detailed anatomical annotations as region labels and map each good unit to its annotation index | Reference code keeps detailed `ccf_label` per neuron | More faithful at unit level than probe insertion target |\n   218→| Fixed task specification | `input_names`, `output_names`, `output_values`, `metadata` | Populate names, class labels, and alignment/binning metadata exactly for the decoder task | N/A | Metadata will explicitly document the requested 50 ms deviation from reference 40 ms / 3.4 ms preprocessing |\n   219→\n   220→### Key Decisions\n   221→1. **Session inclusion**: Use all sessions with at least one good classified unit (`173` sessions). This matches the paper session count and avoids the single raw session with zero analyzable units.\n   222→2. **Trial inclusion**: Keep all trials from included sessions that have full neural coverage for the requested `[-2.5, 1.5)` go-aligned window according to `units/obs_intervals`; within that valid-coverage set, retain stimulation, early-lick, ignore, and miss trials because they are required by the decoder inputs/outputs. This intentionally differs from the paper’s “regular trial” mask, and the difference is required by the user task.\n   223→3. **Neural unit filter**: Use NWB `classification == \"good\"` as the closest NWB-native equivalent of the reference code’s external `goodunits` files.\n   224→4. **Temporal anchor**: Use `go_start_times` as the unique per-trial alignment event. Do not assume one sample/delay event per trial because replayed epochs are explicitly stored in NWB.\n   225→5. **Sample/tone onset definition**: Use the canonical tone onset at `-1.85 s` relative to go cue from the task structure (`0.65 s` sample epoch + `1.2 s` delay), because raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events.\n   226→6. **Input representation**: Both decoder inputs will be fully time-varying arrays of shape `(2, 80)` per trial.\n   227→7. **Output representation**: All outputs will be stored as time-varying arrays of shape `(4, 80)` per trial; choice, outcome, and early-lick labels will be repeated across time, while tongue position varies across bins.\n   228→8. **Ignore-trial choice edge case**: Because most ignore trials have no post-go lick (`13770 / 14095`), choice must use a documented fallback. I will use lick-derived side when available, otherwise instructed side.\n   229→9. **Tongue alignment**: Use last-frame-carried-forward at each bin center to mirror the reference alignment scripts rather than linear interpolation.\n   230→10. **Tongue discretization support set**: Compute per-session percentiles from the aligned tongue-y samples that actually enter the converted dataset, not from unrelated off-trial video periods.\n   231→11. **Brain-region labels**: Use detailed `anno_name` labels per good unit rather than coarse probe targets, because they are unit-resolved and directly stored in NWB.\n   232→12. **Required deviation from reference bins**: Use `50 ms` bins because the decoder task explicitly requires this, while preserving the reference session/unit curation and go-cue alignment principles.\n   233→\n   234→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   235→- [ ] Neural sanity check: for selected session/trial/unit/bin, recompute spike counts directly from raw spike times and verify converted firing rate equals `count / 0.05` with `np.allclose()`.\n   236→- [ ] Input sanity check: for selected stimulated and non-stimulated trials, verify `time_from_tone_onset_s` and `photostim_on` against raw sample/go/stimulation event times with `np.allclose()`.\n   237→- [ ] Output sanity check (trial-level): verify outcome and early-lick labels exactly match raw trial table values for selected trials.\n   238→- [ ] Output sanity check (choice): verify hit/miss trials match lick- or instruction-derived choice, and explicitly inspect ignore-trial fallback behavior on selected trials.\n   239→- [ ] Output sanity check (tongue): for selected session/trial/bin, verify aligned tongue y and discretized class match direct recomputation from raw tracking timestamps/data.\n   240→- [ ] Coverage check: confirm all included sessions have at least 2 trials and all included trials have 80 aligned bins with no NaNs.\n   241→\n   242→---\n   243→\n   244→## Step 6: Script Development\n   245→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   246→\n   247→- Implemented `convert_data.py` with CLI:\n   248→  - `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n   249→  - `--full`\n   250→  - `--sample`\n   251→  - `--show-processing`\n   252→- Main implementation pieces:\n   253→  - NWB loading via `h5py` rather than higher-level wrappers for lower overhead and explicit access to ragged arrays.\n   254→  - Session discovery and exclusion of the single zero-good-unit session.\n   255→  - Good-unit filtering from `units/classification == \"good\"`.\n   256→  - Trial-validity filtering from `units/obs_intervals` so only trials with complete neural coverage for the requested go-aligned window are retained.\n   257→  - Spike binning into `50 ms` go-aligned bins and conversion to firing rate in Hz.\n   258→  - Time-varying decoder inputs for canonical time-from-tone-onset and photostimulation on/off.\n   259→  - Time-varying decoder outputs for choice, outcome, and per-session timing prints.\n   262→\n   263→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   264→- Initial implementation trusted the behavioral trial table directly and therefore processed many trials that were outside the ephys recording intervals, producing all-zero neural matrices and downstream verification warnings.\n   265→- Initial tongue discretization used percentile edges with `np.digitize`, which can collapse the middle class when `q40 == q60` or when many values land exactly on the percentile boundary.\n   266→\n   267→Code speedups added:\n   268→- Added an `obs_intervals`-based valid-trial mask before spike binning, reducing wasted binning work on out-of-recording trials.\n   269→- Kept all heavy operations vectorized in NumPy and used `float16` for stored neural/input arrays and `int16` for outputs to reduce pickle size.\n   270→- Used search-sorted spike binning on per-unit spike vectors rather than per-bin Python loops.\n   271→- Reused aligned bin-center arrays and session-level tongue thresholds instead of recomputing them per trial.\n   272→- Added a residual all-zero-trial check after binning as a defensive guard against edge-case recording coverage mismatches.\n   273→- Replaced percentile-edge discretization with explicit `< q40`, `q40..q60`, `> q60` logic so the middle tongue class remains populated.\n   274→\n   275→---\n   276→\n   277→## Dataset Conversion Notes\n\n## Overview\n- **Dataset**: Brain-wide neural activity underlying memory-guided movement dataset from Steinmetz et al. reference materials provided in this directory\n- **Date started**: 2026-03-23\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `.manifest`\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`\n- `ChenLiuEtAl2023_SpikeSortingQC.pdf`\n- `Dockerfile`\n- `code/`\n- `data/`\n- `datapaper.pdf`\n- `decoder.py`\n- `docker-compose.yaml`\n- `methodpaper.pdf`\n- `methods.txt`\n- `train_decoder.py`\n\nEnvironment checks:\n- `python3` available: `3.13.12`\n- `numpy` import works: `2.3.5`\n- `torch` import works: `2.6.0+cu124`\n- Step 0 checkpoint passed: `ls -la CONVERSION_NOTES.md` confirmed file exists\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `loadmat` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_utils.py` | LOADING | Reads MATLAB exports and recursively converts nested MATLAB structs/cell arrays into Python dictionaries/lists. |\n| `process_one_sess` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | LOADING | Loads all probe files for a session, extracts task/behavior variables, intersects ephys units with histology, and aggregates per-session data across probes. |\n| `sliding_histogram` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Converts per-trial spike times into binned spike counts or firing rates; output firing rates have shape `(n_bins, n_trials, n_neurons)`. |\n| `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | CURATION | Uses QC-selected unit indices plus side assignment from CCF ML coordinate (`5700` midline) to select neurons for each side/region. |\n| `process_one_area` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Truncates spike times to analysis window, bins them into firing rates, and saves per-area session pickle files with behavior/task metadata. |\n| `process_all_sess_parallel` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Batch driver for preprocessing all sessions in parallel. The Sherlock script sets `bw=0.04`, `stride=0.0034`, `begin_time=-3.0`, `end_time=3.0`, `qc_mode='classifier'`. |\n| `load_session` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | LOADING | Re-loads all per-area pickle files from one session and concatenates neurons across files into a single session dictionary. |\n| `get_regular_trial_mask` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | CURATION | Defines “regular trials” as no early lick, no auto water, no free water, correctness not equal to `-1`, and no photostimulation (`stimulation[:,0] == 0`). |\n| `create_4fold_trial_type_mask` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/functions_for_r2.py` | PROCESSING | Builds stratification labels from `trial_type` and `correctness`: hit right, miss right, hit left, miss left; pools misses if some classes are too small. |\n| `temporal_alignment_embed_and_ephys` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/functions_for_r2.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns video-derived embeddings and ephys firing rates onto overlapping time points assuming common timestep `dt=0.0034 s`. |\n\n### Notes\n- `code/README.md` confirms this repository is the analysis code for the 2025 movement-encoding paper and points to processed MAP dataset usage.\n- The primary reference preprocessing path is `code/Sherlock/preprocess_all_ephys.py`, which calls `preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.process_all_sess_parallel(...)`.\n- In `process_one_sess`, behavioral/task variables are read once from the first probe file of a session and then copied to all saved area files. Key variables include `early_lick_trials`, `auto_water_trials`, `free_water_trials`, `lick_directions`, `lick_times`, `correctness`, `gocue_time`, `delay_period`, `sample_period`, `stimulation`, and `trial_type`.\n- Raw lick and stimulation times are explicitly shifted into go-cue coordinates by subtracting per-trial go cue time. Spike times are treated as already go-cue aligned in the exported files.\n- Neuron curation in the reference code is not based on ad hoc thresholds inside the preprocessing script. Instead it relies on externally generated QC files (`goodunits`) and then further requires intersection with histology/unit IDs so only neurons with both ephys and anatomical assignment are retained.\n- The preprocessed session format used throughout the paper stores data per area but downstream analysis concatenates areas back into one session matrix across neurons.\n- Trial filtering for most downstream decoders excludes early-lick, auto-water, free-water, no-response, and stimulation trials. This is specific to the paper’s analyses and may differ from the decoder task here; I need to decide later whether to preserve all trials or reproduce this curation exactly where compatible with the requested outputs.\n- `trial_type` is encoded in the reference preprocessing as `1` for left instruction (`'l'`) and `0` for right instruction (`'r'`).\n- There is an internal comment inconsistency around `lick_directions`: one comment says `0=left, 1=right`, another says `0=right, 1=left`. This must be resolved from raw data and reference texts before final mapping.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` is a DANDI-style NWB dataset (`dandiset.yaml` + one folder per subject).\n- There are `28` subject folders named `sub-<subject_id>`.\n- Each session is a single NWB file inside its subject folder, for a total of `174` NWB files.\n- File naming pattern: `data/sub-<subject>/sub-<subject>_ses-<datetime>_behavior+ecephys[+ogen].nwb`.\n- `dandiset.yaml` identifies the dataset as **Mesoscale Activity Map Dataset** and reports `174` files and `28` subjects, matching the local copy.\n- Representative NWB organization:\n  - `intervals/trials`: trial table with columns `trial`, `photostim_onset`, `photostim_power`, `photostim_duration`, `trial_uid`, `task`, `task_protocol`, `trial_instruction`, `early_lick`, `outcome`, `auto_water`, `free_water`, plus `start_time`/`stop_time`.\n  - `units`: spike-sorted units with metadata columns including `unit_quality`, `classification`, `anno_name`, QC metrics, `is_good_trials`, and ragged `spike_times`.\n  - `acquisition/BehavioralEvents`: event time series for `presample`, `sample`, `delay`, `go`, `trialend`, `left_lick`, `right_lick`, and photostimulation start/stop times.\n  - `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries`: continuous tracked positions. All sessions include `Camera0_side_JawTracking`, `Camera0_side_NoseTracking`, and `Camera0_side_TongueTracking`; some sessions additionally include whisker or lickport tracking.\n- Tongue/jaw/nose tracking time series are shaped `(n_frames, 3)` with columns `(x, y, likelihood)`. Example `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` description: `('tongue_x', 'tongue_y', 'tongue_likelihood')`.\n- Trial value encodings observed directly from NWB tables:\n  - `trial_instruction`: `left` / `right`\n  - `outcome`: `hit` / `miss` / `ignore`\n  - `early_lick`: `early` / `no early`\n  - `task`: `audio delay`\n- Unit annotations:\n  - `classification` contains `good` / `unlabelled` plus occasional `nan`.\n  - `unit_quality` contains `good` / `multi`.\n  - `anno_name` stores detailed Allen-style anatomical labels per unit; examples include `Caudoputamen`, `Secondary motor area, layer 5`, `Mediodorsal nucleus of thalamus`, `Midbrain reticular nucleus`.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 272227 raw units across NWB files |\n| Neurons / session | min 493, mean 1564.52, max 3191 raw units |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | min 3, mean 6.21, max 10 |\n| Trials (total) | 94990 |\n| Trials / session | min 264, mean 545.92, max 800 |\n\nAdditional raw-data statistics:\n- Units with `classification == \"good\"`: `69453` total, min `0`, mean `399.16`, max `923` per session.\n- Trial instruction counts across all raw trials: `right=48913`, `left=46077`.\n- Outcome counts across all raw trials: `hit=65254`, `miss=15641`, `ignore=14095`.\n- Early lick counts across all raw trials: `no early=84185`, `early=10805`.\n- Sessions with tongue tracking: `174/174`.\n- Sessions with at least one non-`N/A` photostimulation trial: `168/174`.\n- Core trial-table schema and core behavioral-event schema are identical in all `174/174` sessions.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 69,943 good units | “69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions” |\n| Neurons / session | ~404 good units/session mean (69943/173) | Derived from paper totals |\n| Subjects | 28 mice | “based on data from 28 mice” |\n| Sessions / subject | 173 behavioral sessions total | “173 behavioral sessions” |\n| Trials (total) | not stated globally in text | Session average reported instead |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130-785 | “average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session” |\n| Neural data time bin | 40 ms bin width; 3.4 ms stride in method paper preprocessing | “binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms” |\n| Behavior data time bin | 300 Hz video tracking | “High-speed videos … were acquired at 300 Hz” |\n| Reward rate | 84% correct control-trial rate, range 65-99% | “84% correct rate (range, 65-99%)” |\n| Photostim trial fraction | ~25% of trials in subset of mice/sessions | “deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials” |\n| Probe insertions | 655 in STAR Methods; figure summary says 660 penetrations | “655 probe insertions” / “660 penetrations” |\n| Good-unit fraction | 25.9% of Kilosort2 clusters | “25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2” |\n| Video->choice AUC before sample | 0.51 ± 0.06 s.d., n=106 sessions | “before the sample epoch … AUC … 0.51 ± 0.06” |\n| Video->choice AUC sample+delay | 0.66 ± 0.12 s.d., n=106 sessions | “In the sample and delay epochs, the mean AUC increased significantly (0.66 ± 0.12)” |\n| Video->choice AUC response | 0.99 ± 0.01 s.d., n=106 sessions | “Soon after the go cue, prediction saturated … 0.99 ± 0.01” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Behavioral task structure from `methods.txt` / datapaper:\n  - Sample epoch: three 150 ms tones with 100 ms inter-tone intervals.\n  - Delay epoch: `1.2 s`.\n  - Go cue: `0.1 s` auditory cue marking end of delay.\n  - Response epoch / answer period: `1.5 s`.\n  - Consumption period: `1.5 s`.\n  - Trial ends after `1.5 s` without licking, followed by `250 ms` ITI.\n- Temporal alignment convention in the reference preprocessing is go-cue-centered. The code subtracts go cue time from lick times and stimulation times, and the papers discuss peri-go-cue timing throughout (`Time to go (s)`).\n- Photoinhibition timing: late-delay ALM photoinhibition lasts the final `0.5 s` of delay including `100 ms` ramp-down and ends before the go cue.\n- Method paper preprocessing for the video/neural analysis:\n  - Spikes converted to firing rates using `40 ms` bins and `3.4 ms` stride.\n  - Only side-view video frames were used.\n  - DeepLabCut markers included jaw, paws, and tongue.\n  - Marker outliers were identified using a five-sigma frame-to-frame velocity rule and imputed from nearby frames.\n  - Tongue position was imputed to its mean when occluded before the response epoch.\n- For PSTHs in the datapaper, spike counts were binned at `1 ms` then boxcar-averaged over `200 ms`; that is for figure generation, not the main preprocessed dataset used by the method paper.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Spike sorting performed with Kilosort2; each cluster assigned 15 quality metrics.\n- Region-specific logistic-regression classifiers were trained from manually curated labels in five major area groups (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla).\n- The classifier outputs defined the “good” units used in the papers.\n- Region mapping from `methods.txt`:\n  - Cortex classifier: ALM, other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory, cortical subplate.\n  - Striatum classifier: striatum, pallidum.\n  - Thalamus classifier: thalamus, hypothalamus.\n  - Midbrain classifier: midbrain, pons.\n  - Medulla classifier: medulla, cerebellum.\n- False alarm rates on held-out manually labeled units: cortex `7.8%`, striatum `6.4%`, thalamus `7.3%`, midbrain `5.5%`, medulla `4.3%`.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Early lick trials and no-response trials were excluded for analysis in the datapaper.\n- Overall behavioral performance for session inclusion was computed on control trials only, excluding early licks.\n- Sessions were selected for analysis with:\n  - performance `> 65%`\n  - at least `50` correct lick-left and `50` correct lick-right trials.\n- Many downstream analyses in the method paper further use only correct trials, or only non-stimulation “regular” trials, depending on the analysis.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, pre-sample | ROC AUC `0.51 ± 0.06` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, sample+delay | ROC AUC `0.66 ± 0.12` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Choice from behavioral videos, response | ROC AUC `0.99 ± 0.01` (n=106 sessions) |\n| Single-neuron choice / movement prediction during delay | ROC AUC used as metric in method paper; neurons considered modulated at AUC `> 0.6` in some analyses |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session count | Reference preprocessing / papers operate on analyzed sessions; code paths and text consistently refer to `173` behavioral sessions | Local NWB release contains `174` raw sessions | Datapaper / methods: `173 behavioral sessions` | Exactly one raw session (`sub-440958_ses-20190216T162508_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb`) has zero units with `classification == good`. Excluding that session yields `173` sessions, matching the papers. |\n| Probe insertion count | Reference text methods: `655 probe insertions`; datapaper figure caption: `660 penetrations` | Raw NWB HDF5 electrode-group count is `659` insertions across 174 sessions; excluding the zero-good-unit session yields `655` | Figure caption says `660 penetrations`, STAR Methods says `655 probe insertions` | Use `655` as the analyzed-set count because it matches the code/paper analyzed session set after excluding the zero-good-unit session. Treat `660` as a figure-caption inconsistency or broader raw-count convention. |\n| Good-unit total | Reference code uses external classifier-produced `goodunits` files; whitepaper/papers report `69,943` good units | NWB `classification == good` gives `69,453` units | Papers: `69,943 good units` | Small residual mismatch of `490` units remains. Most likely causes are release/version differences or a difference between the external `goodunits` files used by the reference code and the NWB `classification` field. For NWB-native conversion, `classification == good` is the closest directly available analogue and matches the session / insertion counts after excluding the zero-good session. I will keep checking this later against decoder behavior and dataset statistics. |\n| Trial filtering | `get_regular_trial_mask` excludes early lick, auto water, free water, no-response, and stimulation trials | Raw trials include all of these categories | Papers describe exclusion of early lick and no-response trials; some downstream analyses also restrict to correct or control-only trials | Distinguish three levels: raw trial table, paper session-selection criteria, and stricter downstream “regular trial” filters. The conversion should preserve requested outputs and only apply exclusions required by the decoder task or by clear reference consistency. |\n| Trial/event alignment | Reference code assumes go-cue-aligned exported spike/behavior variables and stores one trial row per trial | In NWB, `go_start_times` matches trial count exactly, but `sample_start_times` differs in `173/174` sessions and `delay_start_times` differs in `174/174` sessions because replayed epochs are stored explicitly | Papers state that early licks triggered replay of sample/delay epochs | Use `go_start_times` as the unique per-trial alignment anchor. When sample/delay timing is needed, use the final sample/delay event within each trial window rather than assuming one event per trial. |\n| Photostim timing representation | Reference code stores stimulation times relative to go cue after subtracting go cue time | NWB trial table stores `photostim_onset` relative to trial start; in example trials, subtracting `(go_time - trial_start)` gives about `-1.2 s`, matching late-delay stimulation | Papers: photoinhibition in last `0.5 s` of delay, ending before go cue | Convert photostimulation onset/offset from trial-start coordinates into go-aligned coordinates during conversion. |\n| Trial instruction coding | Reference preprocessing stores `trial_type = 1*(trial_type == 'l')` | NWB trial table stores strings `left` / `right` | Papers discuss lick-left / lick-right trial types | Map NWB `trial_instruction` to left/right categorical outputs directly; this is consistent with the reference code once recoded. |\n\nFinal consistency understanding:\n- The NWB files are the raw session-level source.\n- The paper/code analyzed dataset is best approximated in NWB as: sessions with at least one good classified unit, using go-cue-centered alignment, and QC-filtered units corresponding to `classification == good`.\n- `go_start_times` is the reliable one-per-trial event; sample/delay event streams need within-trial disambiguation because of replayed epochs.\n- The method paper’s `40 ms` / `3.4 ms` neural preprocessing describes the reference analysis pipeline, but the requested decoder conversion will intentionally deviate to `50 ms` bins while preserving the same alignment and curation principles where possible.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units/spike_times`, `units/classification`, `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` | `neural` | Keep only units with `classification == \"good\"`; exclude the single zero-good-unit session; bin absolute spike times into go-aligned `[-2.5, 1.5)` windows using `50 ms` bins; convert counts to firing rates in Hz | `process_one_sess`, `process_one_area`, `sliding_histogram` | One trial array per trial, shape `(n_good_units, 80)` |\n| Task structure from papers (`sample = 0.65 s`, `delay = 1.2 s`) + go cue | `input[0]` | Compute `time_from_tone_onset_s = bin_center - (-1.85 s)` for every bin | `process_one_sess` stores sample/delay periods; go-centered alignment throughout reference code | Raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events and are not reliable one-to-one trial markers; using the canonical `-1.85 s` tone onset is more consistent with the task definition |\n| `intervals/trials/photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration`, `start_time`, `go_start_times` | `input[1]` | Convert photostim onset/duration from trial-start coordinates to go-centered coordinates; emit binary `0/1` per bin center for photostim on/off | `process_one_sess` aligns stimulation by subtracting go cue | Keep stimulation trials because photostim is an explicit decoder input |\n| `intervals/trials/trial_instruction`, `intervals/trials/outcome`, lick events | `output[0]` (`choice`) | Encode left=`0`, right=`1`; for `hit`, use instructed side; for `miss`, use opposite side; for `ignore`, use first post-go lick side if present, else first lick side anywhere in trial if present, else fall back to instructed side | `process_one_sess` stores `trial_type`; lick-direction handling is implicit in exported behavior variables | Output is constant across time bins for a trial |\n| `intervals/trials/outcome` | `output[1]` (`outcome`) | Map `ignore -> 0`, `miss -> 1`, `hit -> 2` | Paper/task variable; used in trial filtering logic throughout code | Constant across time bins |\n| `intervals/trials/early_lick` | `output[2]` (`early_lick`) | Map `no early -> 0`, `early -> 1` | `get_regular_trial_mask` and datapaper methods | Constant across time bins |\n| `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking` `(x, y, likelihood)` + timestamps | `output[3]` (`tongue_y_position`) | Clean tracking using method-paper-inspired velocity-outlier interpolation; align to go-centered bin centers using last-frame-carried-forward; compute per-session 40th/60th percentiles over all included aligned bins; discretize y into `0/1/2` | Method paper preprocessing + `align_markers_between_lims` | Time-varying output over 80 bins |\n| `subject` folder / NWB subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Use exact subject IDs (`sub-xxxxx`) and map each session to its subject index | N/A | Session order will follow sorted NWB file paths |\n| `units/anno_name` for good units | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Use unique detailed anatomical annotations as region labels and map each good unit to its annotation index | Reference code keeps detailed `ccf_label` per neuron | More faithful at unit level than probe insertion target |\n| Fixed task specification | `input_names`, `output_names`, `output_values`, `metadata` | Populate names, class labels, and alignment/binning metadata exactly for the decoder task | N/A | Metadata will explicitly document the requested 50 ms deviation from reference 40 ms / 3.4 ms preprocessing |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session inclusion**: Use all sessions with at least one good classified unit (`173` sessions). This matches the paper session count and avoids the single raw session with zero analyzable units.\n2. **Trial inclusion**: Keep all trials from included sessions that have full neural coverage for the requested `[-2.5, 1.5)` go-aligned window according to `units/obs_intervals`; within that valid-coverage set, retain stimulation, early-lick, ignore, and miss trials because they are required by the decoder inputs/outputs. This intentionally differs from the paper’s “regular trial” mask, and the difference is required by the user task.\n3. **Neural unit filter**: Use NWB `classification == \"good\"` as the closest NWB-native equivalent of the reference code’s external `goodunits` files.\n4. **Temporal anchor**: Use `go_start_times` as the unique per-trial alignment event. Do not assume one sample/delay event per trial because replayed epochs are explicitly stored in NWB.\n5. **Sample/tone onset definition**: Use the canonical tone onset at `-1.85 s` relative to go cue from the task structure (`0.65 s` sample epoch + `1.2 s` delay), because raw NWB sample-event streams contain replay-related extra events.\n6. **Input representation**: Both decoder inputs will be fully time-varying arrays of shape `(2, 80)` per trial.\n7. **Output representation**: All outputs will be stored as time-varying arrays of shape `(4, 80)` per trial; choice, outcome, and early-lick labels will be repeated across time, while tongue position varies across bins.\n8. **Ignore-trial choice edge case**: Because most ignore trials have no post-go lick (`13770 / 14095`), choice must use a documented fallback. I will use lick-derived side when available, otherwise instructed side.\n9. **Tongue alignment**: Use last-frame-carried-forward at each bin center to mirror the reference alignment scripts rather than linear interpolation.\n10. **Tongue discretization support set**: Compute per-session percentiles from the aligned tongue-y samples that actually enter the converted dataset, not from unrelated off-trial video periods.\n11. **Brain-region labels**: Use detailed `anno_name` labels per good unit rather than coarse probe targets, because they are unit-resolved and directly stored in NWB.\n12. **Required deviation from reference bins**: Use `50 ms` bins because the decoder task explicitly requires this, while preserving the reference session/unit curation and go-cue alignment principles.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural sanity check: for selected session/trial/unit/bin, recompute spike counts directly from raw spike times and verify converted firing rate equals `count / 0.05` with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input sanity check: for selected stimulated and non-stimulated trials, verify `time_from_tone_onset_s` and `photostim_on` against raw sample/go/stimulation event times with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (trial-level): verify outcome and early-lick labels exactly match raw trial table values for selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (choice): verify hit/miss trials match lick- or instruction-derived choice, and explicitly inspect ignore-trial fallback behavior on selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check (tongue): for selected session/trial/bin, verify aligned tongue y and discretized class match direct recomputation from raw tracking timestamps/data.\n- [ ] Coverage check: confirm all included sessions have at least 2 trials and all included trials have 80 aligned bins with no NaNs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Implemented `convert_data.py` with CLI:\n  - `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n  - `--full`\n  - `--sample`\n  - `--show-processing`\n- Main implementation pieces:\n  - NWB loading via `h5py` rather than higher-level wrappers for lower overhead and explicit access to ragged arrays.\n  - Session discovery and exclusion of the single zero-good-unit session.\n  - Good-unit filtering from `units/classification == \"good\"`.\n  - Trial-validity filtering from `units/obs_intervals` so only trials with complete neural coverage for the requested go-aligned window are retained.\n  - Spike binning into `50 ms` go-aligned bins and conversion to firing rate in Hz.\n  - Time-varying decoder inputs for canonical time-from-tone-onset and photostimulation on/off.\n  - Time-varying decoder outputs for choice, outcome, early lick, and discretized tongue-y position.\n  - Per-session processing visualization saved to `processing_<session_id>.png` in `--show-processing` mode.\n  - Shape/type validation and per-session timing prints.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Initial implementation trusted the behavioral trial table directly and therefore processed many trials that were outside the ephys recording intervals, producing all-zero neural matrices and downstream verification warnings.\n- Initial tongue discretization used percentile edges with `np.digitize`, which can collapse the middle class when `q40 == q60` or when many values land exactly on the percentile boundary.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Added an `obs_intervals`-based valid-trial mask before spike binning, reducing wasted binning work on out-of-recording trials.\n- Kept all heavy operations vectorized in NumPy and used `float16` for stored neural/input arrays and `int16` for outputs to reduce pickle size.\n- Used search-sorted spike binning on per-unit spike vectors rather than per-bin Python loops.\n- Reused aligned bin-center arrays and session-level tongue thresholds instead of recomputing them per trial.\n- Added a residual all-zero-trial check after binning as a defensive guard against edge-case recording coverage mismatches.\n- Replaced percentile-edge discretization with explicit `< q40`, `q40..q60`, `> q60` logic so the middle tongue class remains populated.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 834 |\n| Neurons / session | mean 417, min 375, max 459 |\n| Subjects | 1 (`sub-440956`) |\n| Sessions / subject | 2 |\n| Trials (total) | 370 |\n| Trials / session | 233, 137 |\n| `time_from_tone_onset_s` range | [-0.6, 3.3] |\n| `photostim_on` range | [0.0, 1.0] |\n| `choice` distribution | [0.446, 0.554] |\n| `outcome` distribution | [0.200, 0.000, 0.800] |\n| `early_lick` distribution | [0.941, 0.059] |\n| `tongue_y_position` distribution | [0.067, 0.848, 0.086] |\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n- Generated plots for both sample sessions:\n  - `processing_sub-440956_ses-20190207T120657_behavior+ecephys+ogen.png`\n  - `processing_sub-440956_ses-20190208T133600_behavior+ecephys+ogen.png`\n- The underlying converted sample passed `train_decoder.py --verify-only` with no errors or warnings after the `obs_intervals` trial-coverage fix and the tongue discretization fix.\n- No further anomalies were flagged by the conversion script summaries: both sessions had nonzero neural activity, expected input ranges, and populated tongue classes.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n| | |\n| `obs_intervals` pre-filtering of invalid trials | Removes out-of-recording trials before spike binning; eliminates wasted work and zero-trial warnings |\n| Vectorized spike binning / compact dtypes | They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   126→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   127→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   128→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   129→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   130→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   131→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   132→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   135→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   136→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   137→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   138→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   139→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   140→\n   141→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   142→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   143→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   144→\n   145→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   146→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   147→\n   148→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   149→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   150→\n   151→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   152→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   153→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   154→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   155→\n   156→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   157→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   158→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   159→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   160→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   161→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   162→\n   163→## Python environment\n   164→\n   165→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   166→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   167→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   168→\n   169→## Decoder Reference\n   170→\n   171→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   172→- Optional arguments:\n   173→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   174→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   175→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   176→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   177→\n   178→### Purpose\n   179→\n   180→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   181→1. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\n   257→\n   258→**Goal**: Understand the native data structure\n   259→\n   260→**Actions**:\n   261→- Identify how data is organized (all files, formats, hierarchies)\n   262→- Determine available variables and their meanings\n   263→- Check data dimensions and data types\n   264→- Look for documentation or README files\n   265→- Note the data organization and structure\n   266→- Note dataset size parameters such as the **total number** of subjects (mice), sessions, trials, and neurons\n   267→\n   268→**Done when**: You have documented the data structure, file formats, size, and available variables in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 2\".\n   269→\n   270→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 3, update Step 2 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   271→\n   272→---\n   273→\n   274→### Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   275→\n   276→**Goal**: Extract key information from the papers and methods.\n   277→\n   278→**Actions**:\n   279→- Scan the reference papers and methods.txt for important details about the data\n   280→- Look for and note to file:\n   281→  - Any information about dataset size (number of neurons, subjects, sessions, trials)\n   282→  - Fraction of data with different values, e.g. fraction of rewarded outcomes\n   283→  - Data processing, curation, and filtering\n   284→  - Variables important to experiments\n   285→  - Temporal alignment\n   286→  - Temporal binning\n   287→- Read the reference papers to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\n   288→\n   289→**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 3\".\n   290→\n   291→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   292→\n   293→---\n   294→\n   295→### Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   296→\n   297→**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \n   298→\n   299→**Actions**:\n   300→- Compare your understanding of:\n   301→  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\n   302→  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\n   303→  - Analysis described in the reference texts\n   304→- Note and investigate any discrepancies\n   305→- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\n   306→  - Experimental and behavioral variables\n   307→  - Data processing\n   308→  - Data curation\n   309→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   310→\n   311→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   312→\n   313→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   314→\n   315→---\n   316→\n   317→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   318→\n   319→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   320→\n   321→**Actions**:\n   322→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   323→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   324→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   325→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   326→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   327→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   328→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   329→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   330→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   331→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   332→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   333→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   334→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   335→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   336→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   337→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   338→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   339→\n   340→**Done when**:\n   341→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   342→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   343→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   344→\n   345→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   346→\n   347→---\n   348→\n   349→### Step 6: Script Development\n   350→\n   351→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   352→\n   353→**Actions**:\n   354→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   355→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   356→- Include options:\n   357→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   358→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   359→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   360→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   361→- Write efficient code:\n   362→  - Vectorize loops\n   363→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   364→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   365→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   366→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   367→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   368→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   369→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   370→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   371→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   372→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   373→\n   374→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   375→\n   376→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   377→\n   378→---\n   379→\n   380→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   381→\n   382→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   383→\n   384→**Actions**:\n   385→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   386→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   387→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   388→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   389→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   390→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   391→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   392→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   393→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   394→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   395→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   396→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   397→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   398→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   399→\n   400→**Done when**: \n   401→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   402→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   403→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   404→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   405→\n   406→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   407→\n   408→---\n   409→\n   410→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   411→\n   412→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   413→\n   414→**Actions**:\n   415→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   416→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   417→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   418→\n   419→**Investigate any issues**:\n   420→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   421→\n   422→**Done when**: \n   423→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   424→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   425→- Accuracies are above chance\n   426→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   427→\n   428→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   429→\n   430→---\n   431→\n   432→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   433→\n   434→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   435→\n   436→**Actions**:\n   437→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   438→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   439→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   440→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   441→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   442→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   443→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   444→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   445→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   446→\n   447→**Done when**:\n   448→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   449→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   450→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   451→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   452→\n   453→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   454→\n   455→---\n   456→\n   457→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   458→\n   459→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   460→\n   461→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   462→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   463→\n   464→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   465→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   466→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   467→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   468→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   469→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   470→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   471→\n   472→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   473→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   474→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   475→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   476→- Fix any mismatches. \n   477→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   478→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   479→\n   480→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   481→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n   482→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   483→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   484→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   485→\n   486→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   487→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   488→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   489→\n   490→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   491→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   492→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   493→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   494→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   495→\n   496→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   497→\n   498→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   499→\n   500→---\n   501→\n   502→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   503→\n   504→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   505→\n   506→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   507→\n   508→**Actions**:\n   509→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   510→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   511→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   512→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n   513→\n   514→**Done when**: \n   515→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   516→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   517→\n   518→---\n   519→\n   520→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   521→\n   522→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   523→\n   524→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   525→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   526→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   527→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   528→\n   529→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n   530→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n   531→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\n   532→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   533→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   534→\n   535→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   536→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   537→\n   538→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   539→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   540→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   541→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   542→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   543→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n   544→\n   545→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   546→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   547→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   548→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   549→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   550→\n   551→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   552→\n   553→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   554→\n   555→---\n   556→\n   557→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   558→\n   559→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   560→\n   561→**Actions**:\n   562→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   563→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   564→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   565→  - Validation results and tables\n   566→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   567→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   568→  - Dataset description\n   569→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   570→  - Output format specification\n   571→  - Key statistics\n   572→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   573→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   574→\n   575→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   576→\n   577→---\n   578→\n   579→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   580→\n   581→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0.
